# Minimal models and Mori fibre spaces for generalized log canonical Q-pairs

Coordinate `EN-103-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-C-TO-M-0103` | `PM-036.3` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Minimal-models-and-Mori-fibre-spaces-for-generalized-log-canonical-Q-pairs-September-24-2026` | date 2026-09-24 | derived from the openai/math preprint Minimal-models-and-Mori-fibre-spaces-for-generalized-log-canonical-Q-pairs-September-24-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We resolve the existence form of the minimal-model conjecture for projective generalized log canonical ℚ-pairs over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero. Such a pair admits a minimal model when its adjoint divisor is pseudo-effective, and a Mori fibre space otherwise, while keeping the nef ℚ-Cartier b-divisor fixed. Taking the nef part to be zero gives the corresponding result for ordinary log canonical ℚ-pairs.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We resolve the existence form of the minimal-model conjecture for projective generalized log canonical ℚ-pairs over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero. Such a pair admits a minimal model when its adjoint divisor is pseudo-effective, and a Mori fibre space otherwise, while keeping the nef ℚ-Cartier b-divisor fixed. Taking the nef part to be zero gives the corresponding result for ordinary log canonical ℚ-pairs.

- **claim-main-existence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $(X,B+M)$ be a projective generalized log canonical $\mathbb Q$-pair. Thus $X$ is normal and integral, $B$ is an effective rational boundary, and $M$ is the trace of a nef rational Cartier divisor on a higher projective birational model, with $K_X+B+M$ $\mathbb Q$-Cartier.* *There are a normal integral projective variety $Y$ and a birational map $\phi\colon X\dashrightarrow Y$ whose inverse contracts no divisors such that, writing $B_Y=\phi_*B$ and taking $M_Y$ to be the trace of the same nef b-divisor, the generalized pair $(Y,B_Y+M_Y)$ is log canonical and $$A_{X,B+M}(E)\le A_{Y,B_Y+M_Y}(E)$$ for every prime divisor $E$ over their common function field. The inequality is strict for every divisor on $X$ contracted by $\phi$. More precisely, the following alternatives are determined by $D=K_X+B+M$:* 1. *if $D$ is pseudo-effective, then $K_Y+B_Y+M_Y$ is nef;* 2. *if $D$ is not pseudo-effective, there is a projective contraction $h\colon Y\to T$ to a normal projective variety with connected fibres such that $$\dim T<\dim Y,\qquad \rho(Y/T)=1, \qquad -(K_Y+B_Y+M_Y)\text{ is ample over }T.$$
  - Explanation: Every projective generalized log canonical pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero has a birational model that is a minimal model when the adjoint is pseudo-effective, and a Mori fibre space otherwise, with the nef data held fixed and discrepancies never decreasing.
- **claim-ordinary-lc** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-ordinary)): Let $(X,B)$ be a projective log canonical $\mathbb Q$-pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. There is a birational map $\phi:X\dashrightarrow Y$ extracting no divisors, with $B_Y=\phi_*B$, such that $(Y,B_Y)$ is log canonical and $$A_{X,B}(E)\leq A_{Y,B_Y}(E)$$ for every prime divisor over the common function field, strictly for prime divisors on $X$ contracted by $\phi$. If $K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective, $K_Y+B_Y$ is nef; otherwise $Y$ admits a Mori fibre contraction with relative Picard number one and relatively ample $-(K_Y+B_Y)$.
  - Explanation: The ordinary log canonical case follows from the main existence theorem by taking the fixed nef data to be zero.
- **claim-local-phase** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-phase)): Let $(V_i,o_i)$ be a sequence of pointed algebraic Gorenstein klt germs of a fixed dimension $n\geq2$, with $o_i$ closed. Suppose that a finite cyclic group $G_i$ acts on an affine representative of each germ, fixes $o_i$, and admits a semi-invariant canonical generator $\theta_i$, a volume form with zero divisor near $o_i$. Choose an arbitrary element $\gamma_i\in G_i$ for each $i$.* *After passage to a subsequence, there are divisorial valuations $w'_i$ centred at $o_i$, a constant $C>0$, and a positive integer $\ell$, independent of $i$ on that subsequence, such that $$A_{\theta_i}(w'_i)\leq C$$ and, for every nonzero rational semi-invariant function $h$ on $V_i$ of character $\chi$, $$\label{glob:phase-congruence} w'_i(h)\equiv\ell\,\mathop{\mathrm{angle}}\bigl(\chi(\gamma_i)\bigr) \pmod{\mathbb Z}.$$ In particular, $w'_i$ restricts to an integral-valued valuation of $\mathbb C(V_i)^{G_i}$.
  - Explanation: For a sequence of Gorenstein klt germs with cyclic symmetry, a subsequence carries divisorial valuations of bounded discrepancy whose values on semi-invariants reproduce character angles up to one fixed integer.
- **claim-smooth-termination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-smooth-termination)): Assume Theorem <a href="#thm:phase" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:phase">3</a>. Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex variety with pseudo-effective canonical divisor. A $K_X$-MMP with scaling of an effective ample rational divisor $H$, chosen so that $(X,H)$ is klt and $K_X+H$ is ample, terminates with nef canonical divisor. Consequently, on a smooth projective birational model $p:U\to X$ one has $$p^*K_X=P+N, \qquad P\text{ nef and rational Cartier},\quad N\geq0 \text{ rational Cartier}.$$
  - Explanation: Granting the local phase assertion, the canonical program with scaling on a smooth projective variety with pseudo-effective canonical divisor terminates with a nef canonical divisor, giving a decomposition into nef and effective parts.
- **claim-relative-wzd** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-relative-wzd)): Assume Theorem <a href="#thm:phase" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:phase">3</a>. Let $X$ be a smooth complex quasi-projective variety, projective over a normal quasi-projective variety $Z$. If $K_X$ is pseudo-effective over $Z$, it admits an NQC weak Zariski decomposition over $Z$ with rational Cartier parts.
  - Explanation: The smooth absolute termination is transferred to the relative setting through a branched double cover, giving a relative weak Zariski decomposition with rational Cartier parts.
- **claim-field-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-field-descent)): The conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> over $\mathbb C$ implies its conclusion over every algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.
  - Explanation: The conclusion over the complex numbers descends step by step to any algebraically closed field of characteristic zero by spreading out and specialization.
- **claim-cone-estimates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-cone-estimates)): For the preceding choices, every nonzero homogeneous $h_I\in R_I$ of positive degree satisfies $$\label{cone:alpha} \mathop{\mathrm{lct}}_o(C_0;h_I)\geq\frac{1}{2n(I/a)}.$$ Every nonzero regular local function $g$ on $V$ and every divisorial or real quasi-monomial valuation $w$ centred at $o$ satisfy $$\label{cone:alpha-germ} w(g)\leq 2n A_{\theta_V}(w)\,v(g).$$ Moreover, for every valuation $w$ centred at $o$, $$\label{cone:comparison} w(g)\geq s(w)v(g)\quad(g\in\mathcal O_{V,o}), \qquad s(w)=\min_i\frac{w(h_i)}{v(h_i)}.$$ In particular each positive normalized homogeneous degree is at least $1/(2n)$.
  - Explanation: Log canonical thresholds of homogeneous elements on the cone, and valuation comparison bounds on the germ, are controlled uniformly in terms of the dimension and the discrepancy.
- **claim-degree-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-product)): After passage to a subsequence there is a uniform constant $C$ such that $$\label{prod:product-bound} h\,d_1\cdots d_n\leq C, \qquad h=\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(v).$$
  - Explanation: Along a subsequence the volume of the reference valuation times the product of the successive degree scales is uniformly bounded.
- **claim-integral-jump** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-integral-jump)): Fix a positive dimension. Consider a sequence of smooth projective varieties $F$ over algebraically closed fields isomorphic to $\mathbb C$, with projective birational morphisms $F\to F_0$ to normal varieties. Let $\eta_F$ be an lc rational volume form, and let $L$ be the pullback of a big semiample effective $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $F_0$. Suppose the supports of $\operatorname{div}(\eta_F)$ and $L$ on $F$ have simple normal crossings. Write $l_w=w(L)$ and assume the following conditions.* 1. *For every divisorial valuation $w$, the equality $A_{\eta_F}(w)=0$ implies $l_w>0$.* 2. *For every prime divisor $P$ on $F_0$, $$A_{\eta_F}(P)=1\quad\text{if }l_P=0, \qquad A_{\eta_F}(P)\leq\epsilon l_P\quad\text{if }l_P>0,$$ where $\epsilon\to0$ along the sequence.* 3. *For every rational $0<b\leq1/10$, $h^0(F,K_F+\lceil bL\rceil)=1$.* 4. *There is a uniform constant $C$ such that every effective $\mathbb Q$-divisor $J\sim_{\mathbb Q}L$ satisfies $w(J)\leq C l_w$ at every divisorial valuation with $A_{\eta_F}(w)=0$.* *Then, after passage to a subsequence, there is a uniform constant $C'$ such that every such $J$ and every divisorial valuation $w$ satisfy $$\label{prod:integral-jump-bound} w(J)\leq C'\bigl(A_{\eta_F}(w)+l_w\bigr).$$ It suffices that the hypotheses hold sufficiently far along the sequence.
  - Explanation: Under the stated discrepancy and section-count conditions, every effective divisor equivalent to L has valuation bounded by a uniform multiple of discrepancy plus the valuation of L.
- **lemma-graph-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prod-graph-bound)): At every prime divisor $P$ of $F_0$, with its primitive valuation, put $c_P=P(D_F)$. Then $$\label{prod:graph-estimate} \begin{aligned} c_P>0&\quad\Longrightarrow\quad A_{\eta_F}(P)\leq \left(\sum_{j\leq m}e_j-1\right)c_P,\\ c_P=0&\quad\Longrightarrow\quad A_{\eta_F}(P)=1. \end{aligned}$$ This includes the case $m=1$.
  - Explanation: On the normalized graph, the discrepancy of a prime divisor is bounded by the sum of degrees of the pencil generators times its coefficient when positive, and equals one otherwise.
- **lemma-one-section** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prod-one-section)): Suppose $q>0$ and $T=d_{m+1}$ begins the tier after the chosen complete prefix. Uniformly sufficiently far along that jump, $$\label{prod:adjoint-unique} h^0\bigl(F,K_F+\lceil bTD_F\rceil\bigr)=1 \qquad(0<b\leq1/10,\ b\in\mathbb Q).$$
  - Explanation: Just before a degree jump the adjoint linear system on the geometric generic component has exactly one section.
- **lemma-window-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-val-window)): Put $d_{n+1}=+\infty$. There is a uniform constant $C_W$ such that $$\label{val:window-bound} \mathcal H(T):=\dim_{k_0}\mathcal O_{V,o}/(v>T) \le C_W\prod_{j\le m}\frac{T}{d_j} \quad(d_m\le T<d_{m+1},\ 1\le m\le n).$$
  - Explanation: The colength of valuation cutoff ideals on a finite window of degrees is bounded by a uniform constant times the product of the ratios of the cutoff to the degree scales.
- **lemma-gauss-tools** (theorem, [source](#stmt-val-gauss-tools)): The following facts hold for an $n$-dimensional function field in characteristic zero.* 1. *If $w$ is Gauss on a full tuple $t_1,\ldots,t_n$, then it is quasi-monomial, and $$\label{val:log-frame} A_\eta(w)=w\!\left( \frac{\eta}{d\log t_1\wedge\cdots\wedge d\log t_n}\right).$$ This identity is compatible with finite field extension and pullback of the form.* 2. *Suppose $w$ is quasi-monomial and Gauss on a partial tuple $f$. For nearby assignments of real weights to $f$, there are quasi-monomial Gauss extensions whose discrepancies and values on any prescribed finite subset of $L^*$ tend to those of $w$. If $w$ is centred at $o$, the extensions can be kept centred there.* 3. *On the finite cone complex of a smooth proper SNC model, the evaluation of a fixed rational function is continuous, homogeneous, and rational piecewise linear, including on faces.
  - Explanation: Gauss valuations on a full tuple are quasi-monomial with a log-frame discrepancy formula, admit continuous continuations in the weights, and evaluate rational functions piecewise linearly on cone complexes.
- **lemma-strict-retraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-val-strict-retraction)): Let $(Q,D)$ be a smooth proper model with reduced SNC boundary containing $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\operatorname{div}_Q(\eta)$. Retraction of a quasi-monomial valuation $w$ to the boundary cone complex decreases $A_\eta$ by $A_{Q,D}(w)\ge0$, and the decrease is strict unless $w$ already belongs to that complex.
  - Explanation: Retracting a quasi-monomial valuation to the boundary cone complex lowers the form discrepancy, strictly unless the valuation is already on the complex.
- **claim-valuation-optimization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-val-optimization)): The minimum is attained on a finite rational polyhedral subdivision of the SNC cone complex of a fixed smooth model. There are finitely many invariant regular functions, called detectors, such that maximizing the sum of their values among the minimizers permits the choice of a vertex. At this selected vertex, $$\label{val:coefficient-vectors} w(h)=m_h\cdot b\quad(h\in L^*),\qquad A_\eta(w)=e\cdot b,\qquad s(w)=g_s\cdot b,$$ where all coefficient vectors are rational and have bounded denominators for this fixed vertex. Its rational rank is $r$. For an active generator, meaning $w(h_i)=s(w)v_i$, one has $m_{h_i}=v_i g_s$.* *For fixed germ and tuple, selection is described by finitely many piecewise semialgebraic branches as $b$ varies. On a segment starting at independent weights, these branches have a finite partition into intervals on which the selected ratio is rational-smooth. At dependent weights the polyhedral prescription is initially relaxed, without an extra Gauss condition. A compact limit of bounded-ratio selected vertices from independent positive weights remains a centred quasi-monomial Gauss extension at the limiting weights.
  - Explanation: The ratio of discrepancy to comparison factor attains its minimum at a rational vertex of a finite polyhedral subdivision, selected by detector functions.
- **claim-cartier-output** (theorem, [source](#stmt-car-output)): There is a spread of the algebraic data, retaining the given weights and cutoffs, such that, for all sufficiently large reductions of characteristic $p$, there is a single product $H$ of regular eigenfunctions with $$\sigma=m_H=p\phi g_s+O(1).$$ The error depends on the fixed characteristic-zero data but not on $p$. For this $H$ and every vector of nonnegative integers $(j_\nu)$, there are a regular nonzero eigenfunction $R$ and integers $0\le k_i<p$ with $$\begin{aligned} p m_R&=\sigma+\sum_\nu j_\nu m_{t_\nu}+k-(p-1)e, \label{car:C2-value}\\ p\chi_R&=\chi_H+\sum_\nu j_\nu\chi_{t_\nu} +\chi_{f^k}-(p-1)\chi_\eta. \label{car:C2-character} \end{aligned}$$ Characters are written additively. For every preassigned cutoff $T$, any collection of these reduced regular eigenfunctions with distinct pairs <span class="upright">(value, character)</span> and weights at most $s(w_{\rm orig})T$ has size at most the characteristic-zero number $\mathcal H(T)$ of Lemma <a href="#val:window" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="val:window">12</a>.
  - Explanation: After reduction to large positive characteristic, the logarithmic Cartier operator produces regular eigenfunctions whose values and characters satisfy exact affine relations with bounded counts.
- **lemma-strict-inequality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-car-strict-inequality)): For every divisorial $z$ one has $$\label{car:strict} A_\rho(z)+\phi l(z)\ge0.$$ The inequality is strict when $z$ is nonnegative on $A_0$.
  - Explanation: The residual form discrepancy plus the optimal ratio times the generator decay rate is nonnegative, and strictly positive on valuations nonnegative on the affine residue model.
- **lemma-algebraic-counts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-round-algebraic-counts)): For regular eigenfunctions $t$ whose $w$-initial is algebraic over the graded tuple field of $f$, put $$\Lambda_f=\left\langle(\mathbf e_i,\chi_{f_i}):1\le i\le r \right\rangle_{\mathbb Z} \subset\mathbb Q^r\times\operatorname{Hom}(G,k_0^*),$$ where $\mathbf e_i=m_{f_i}$ is the $i$th standard vector. Then $$\begin{aligned} \#\{(m_t,\chi_t)\bmod\Lambda_f\}&\le C_3, \label{round:U1}\\ \|m_{t,i}\|&\le C_4\frac{w(t)}{d_i}\quad(1\le i\le r). \label{round:U2} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Exponent-character classes of eigenfunctions with algebraic initials are uniformly finite in number and their exponent coordinates are controlled by the weight over the degree scale.
- **lemma-enlarge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-round-enlarge)): If $r<m$, there is a centred regular eigenfunction $t$ with $w(t)\le C_5d$ whose initial is transcendental over the graded tuple field. Adjoining $t$ preserves the Gauss property at $w$. The enlarged tuple can then be continued to arbitrarily close independent assignments, with ratio increased by at most one.
  - Explanation: When the tuple is incomplete within a tier, a function of controlled weight with transcendental initial exists and can be adjoined preserving the Gauss property.
- **lemma-derivative** (theorem, [source](#stmt-round-derivative)): When $r=m$, the selected vertex satisfies $$\label{round:U3} \left\|\frac{(e-\phi g_s)_i}{s}\right\| \le C_6\frac d{d_i}\quad(1\le i\le m).$$ Consequently, on a selected ratio branch along a segment of weights, at its independent parameters, $$\label{round:derivative-bound} \left\|\frac{d\Phi}{dt}\right\| \le C_6\sum_{i\le m}\frac d{d_i}\|\dot b_i\|.$$
  - Explanation: At a complete tuple the derivative of the ratio along a weight segment is bounded by the weighted variation of the coordinates.
- **lemma-segment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-round-segment)): For the bounds fixed above, choose $S$ large enough that $C_6C_7/S<1$. If a completed prefix at the start of the segment has a Gauss extension with an available ratio bound $M$, its target endpoint has a centred quasi-monomial Gauss extension with ratio at most $M+1$.
  - Explanation: For a large enough common rounding integer, the exact target weights admit a Gauss extension whose ratio exceeds the available bound by at most one.
- **claim-lifting-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ij-mixing-item)): Fix positive integers $d,r$ and a positive real number $C$. The following assertions hold.* 1. * Let $P/Q$ be a regular extension with $\operatorname{trdeg}_{\mathbb C}P\le d$, having a projective Fano type generic model. Let $\sigma,\psi$ be forms on $P$ whose boundaries on this model are effective, with $\sigma$ of index at most $r$. Suppose, on every nonzero divisorial valuation of $P$, that $$A_\psi>0,\qquad 0\le A_\sigma\le C A_\psi.$$ For every $\lambda>0$ there is $\delta=\delta(d,r,C,\lambda)>0$ with the following property. If $u$ is a nonzero integral-valued divisorial valuation of $Q$ and $$\inf_{w\|_Q=u}A_\psi(w)<\delta,$$ then there is an integral-valued divisorial valuation $v$ of $P$ whose restriction is a positive multiple of $u$ and for which $A_\psi(v)<\lambda$. In relative dimension zero the regular extension is $P=Q$, and the same assertion holds.* 2. *Let $P$ have a projective Fano type model over $\mathbb C$ of dimension at most $d$. Let $\sigma,\psi,\chi$ be forms with effective boundaries on that model, with $\sigma$ of index at most $r$, and suppose $$A_\psi>0,\qquad 0\le A_\sigma\le C A_\psi.$$ Suppose in addition that, for some $c>0$, $$A_\chi(w)\ge c A_\psi(w) \quad\text{whenever }w\ne0\text{ and }A_\sigma(w)=0.$$ There is a rational number $a\in(0,1)$ depending only on $d,r,C,c$ such that $$(1-a)A_\psi(w)+aA_\chi(w)>0 \quad\text{for every nonzero divisorial }w.$$* *All infima and homogeneous inequalities allow positive rational scalings of prime divisorial valuations. An integral-valued valuation is not required to be primitive.
  - Explanation: Integral valuations of the base lift with controlled discrepancy, and a convex mixture of forms stays strictly positive, with constants depending only on the stated numerical data.
- **claim-bounded-fibre** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tower-bounded-mixture)): Let $L/K$ be a regular extension of finitely generated fields over $\mathbb C$, with $\operatorname{trdeg}_{\mathbb C}L\le d$. Suppose it has a positive-dimensional projective generic model $G/K$ that is geometrically $\epsilon_0$-lc Fano, where $\epsilon_0>0$ is fixed: $-K_G$ is ample and $\mathbb Q$-Cartier, and the geometric underlying variety is $\epsilon_0$-lc. Let $\sigma$ be a form on $L$ of index at most $r$, with effective boundary on $G$ and with $A_\sigma\ge0$ on all absolute divisorial valuations of $L$. For every nonzero divisorial valuation $u$ of $K$, set $$\bar A_\sigma(u)=\inf_{w\|_K=u}A_\sigma(w),$$ and use the same notation for other forms.* 1. * There is a form $\sigma_K$ on $K$, with index bounded only in terms of $d,\epsilon_0,r$, such that $$\tfrac12\bar A_\sigma(u) \le A_{\sigma_K}(u)\le\bar A_\sigma(u) \quad\text{for every nonzero divisorial }u.$$* 2. * Let $\psi$ be a form on $L$ with effective boundary on $G$, $A_\psi>0$ on all nonzero absolute divisorial valuations, and $A_\sigma\le C A_\psi$. For every $\lambda>0$, there is $\delta>0$, depending only on $d,\epsilon_0,r,C,\lambda$, such that an integral-valued nonzero divisorial valuation $u$ of $K$ with $\bar A_\psi(u)<\delta$ has an integral-valued divisorial lift $v$ on $L$ satisfying $$v\|_K\in\mathbb Q_{>0}u,\qquad A_\psi(v)<\lambda.$$* 3. *Let $\chi,\psi$ be forms with effective boundaries on $G$; neither is required to be absolutely lc in this part. Suppose that, for a fixed $c>0$, $$A_\chi(w)\ge cA_\psi(w) \quad\text{on all nonzero divisorial }w \text{ with }A_\sigma(w)=0.$$ There is $D\ge0$, depending only on $d,\epsilon_0,r,c$, such that $$\label{tower:bounded-mixture-inequality} A_\chi(w)\ge cA_\psi(w)-D A_\sigma(w)$$ whenever $w$ is trivial on $K$, or $u=w\|_K$ is nonzero and $\bar A_\sigma(u)=0$.* *The boundaries on $G$ are interpreted by dividing out a base volume form as above. All comparisons allow positive rational scalings, and integral-valued valuations need not be primitive. If $K=\mathbb C$, the first part has no nonzero valuations to test and a constant form of index one suffices; the second part is vacuous, while the third part includes every nonzero divisorial valuation of $L$.
  - Explanation: For bounded Fano generic fibres, a bounded-index base form approximates the barred discrepancy, integral valuations lift, and a mixture inequality holds with a uniform constant.
- **lemma-base-substitution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tower-base-substitution)): Let $L/K$ be a regular extension as above, and let $G/K$ be a normal geometrically integral projective generic model. Let $\omega$ be a form whose boundary on $G$ is effective and sub-lc. Then the threshold canonical bundle formula gives a discriminant b-divisor and a b-Cartier b-semiample moduli b-divisor with generalized discrepancy $\bar A_\omega$. Each defining infimum for a nonzero divisorial base valuation is finite and attained.* *Suppose another such form $\psi$ has $\bar A_\psi(u)>0$ for every nonzero divisorial $u$ of $K$. For every rational $\gamma>0$, there is a form $\omega_K$ on $K$ such that $$\label{tower:substitution-bounds} \bar A_\omega(u)-\gamma\bar A_\psi(u) \le A_{\omega_K}(u)\le\bar A_\omega(u).$$ No index bound is asserted. The boundary of $\omega$ need not be sub-lc at vertical valuations.
  - Explanation: The canonical bundle formula yields base forms whose discrepancy lies between the barred discrepancy minus a multiple of the anchor and the barred discrepancy, without bounding the index.
- **lemma-geometric-step** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tower-geometric-step)): Let $P/Q$ be a regular extension of positive transcendence degree having a projective Fano type model over $Q$. Suppose finitely many forms on $P$ have effective boundaries on this model. There is a projective Mori contraction $H\to U$ over $Q$, with $Q(H)=P$ and $R=Q(U)$, such that:* 1. *$P/R$ and $R/Q$ are regular, and $\operatorname{trdeg}_Q R<\operatorname{trdeg}_Q P$;* 2. *the generic fibre of $H\to U$ is positive-dimensional and geometrically $1/10$-lc Fano;* 3. *$H$ and $U$ are of Fano type over $Q$, and all the carried form boundaries remain effective on $H$.* *In addition, any base substitutions with discrepancy at most the appropriate barred discrepancy have effective traces on $U$.
  - Explanation: A regular extension with a Fano type model admits a Mori contraction with positive-dimensional Fano generic fibre, lowering transcendence degree while carried boundaries stay effective.
- **claim-preparation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bounded-preparation)): The finitely many packages can be chosen with projective morphisms $f:Y\to B$ having the following properties. The base is smooth and strict toroidal; the source is strict simplicial toroidal; and every source cone maps onto a base cone. The good parameter open is disjoint from the base boundary. For each field-valued point used in a package, $Y_K$ maps projectively and birationally to the prescribed $G$, and the differential and form identities specialize. The divisor of $\eta$, regarded as a relative logarithmic pluri-top form, is toroidal. Arbitrary prescribed proper closed subsets can be included in the boundary during this construction.
  - Explanation: Finitely many toroidal packages with surjective cone maps and a toroidal relative form divisor cover every parameter used.
- **remark-saturation-needed** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): The preliminary saturation and the subsequent choice of labels cannot be omitted. On the open set $y(y^2+x)\ne0$, the map $$t=x^2(y^2+x)$$ has boundary lattice map multiplication by two at the ordinary component $x=0$. Its generic fibre is geometrically integral: with $z=xy$, its equation is $z^2=t-x^3$. After $t=\pi^2$, normalization introduces $q=\pi/x$, with $$q^2=y^2+x,\qquad \pi=xq.$$ The special fibre over that old component has two primes, $q=y$ and $q=-y$. Both have boundary weights $w(x)=w(\pi)=1$, $w(y)=0$, but their orders differ on $q-y$. The bounded Kummer normalization separates them into different new strata, each with primitive boundary map. Taking the labels on that new model is exactly what makes the uniqueness proved above applicable.
  - Explanation: An explicit family shows that without saturation and labelling two special-fibre primes share boundary weights but give different orders.
- **claim-trait-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-trait-comparison)): For every parameter map allowed above and every nonzero divisorial base valuation $u$, the barred reference discrepancy is $$\bar A_\sigma(u)=A_\zeta(u)+\mu(b). \tag{T}\label{trait:T}$$ For a divisorial $w$ over $u$, with labelled monomial retraction $w_x$, there is an excess satisfying $$A_\sigma(w)=A_\zeta(u)+\phi(x)+E(w),\qquad E(w)\ge0,\qquad E(w_x)=0. \tag{TE}\label{trait:TE}$$ Let $\omega$ be any other form with effective boundary on $G$, and compare it to $\sigma$ at a common tensor index $p$, writing $$A_\omega(v)=A_\sigma(v)+\frac{v(H)}p.$$ There is a uniform constant $C_2$ such that $$\begin{split} \|w(H)-w_x(H)\|&\le C_2pE(w),\\ \|w_x(H)-w_{x'}(H)\|&\le C_2p\\|x-x'\\|. \end{split} \tag{TV}\label{trait:TV}$$ The second inequality holds along every segment in one labelled slice cell, including incident endpoints, in fixed cone coordinates. The rational monomial valuations in these assertions are the compatible algebraic labelled ray orders constructed above. Formulae <a href="#trait:T" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trait:T">[trait:T]</a> and <a href="#trait:TE" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trait:TE">[trait:TE]</a> do not require absolute log canonicity of $\sigma$.
  - Explanation: The barred reference discrepancy equals the base discrepancy plus the toroidal minimum, and any prolongation differs from its monomial retraction by a nonnegative excess with Lipschitz control.
- **lemma-effective-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inc-effective-model)): There is a smooth projective toroidal resolution $P\to Y$ and a finite sequence of ordinary $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt MMP steps over $B$ from $P$ to a model $P_j$ with an effective dlt boundary $D_j$ such that $$K_{P_j}+D_j\sim_{\mathbb Q,B}0,$$ and $(P_j,D_j)$ is crepant to $(Y,C^\natural)$. The lc centres of the crepant subpair on $P$ and of $(P_j,D_j)$ correspond by strict transform, preserving their inclusions and their proper images in $B$.
  - Explanation: A finite dlt program over the base reaches a model with effective dlt boundary and trivial relative adjoint, preserving lc centres and their incidences.
- **claim-lc-incidence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-lc-incidence)): Let $s$ have nonzero base cone $\tau_s$. Let $\delta_0$ be a source face on which $g$ vanishes, let $V=\overline{O_{\delta_0}}$ be its closed stratum, and let $C_s$ be any connected component of $V_s$. The zero face is allowed, with $V=Y$. Then there is a source cone $\beta$ such that $$g\|_\beta=0,\qquad p_*(\beta)=\tau_s,\qquad \overline{O_\beta}\subset V,\qquad O_\beta\cap C_s\ne\varnothing.$$
  - Explanation: Every connected component of a zero-gap closed stratum fibre meets the stratum of a zero-gap cone mapping onto the base cone.

```json
        "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $(X,B+M)$ be a projective generalized log canonical $\\mathbb Q$-pair. Thus $X$ is normal and integral, $B$ is an effective rational boundary, and $M$ is the trace of a nef rational Cartier divisor on a higher projective birational model, with $K_X+B+M$ $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier.*\n\n*There are a normal integral projective variety $Y$ and a birational map $\\phi\\colon X\\dashrightarrow Y$ whose inverse contracts no divisors such that, writing $B_Y=\\phi_*B$ and taking $M_Y$ to be the trace of the same nef b-divisor, the generalized pair $(Y,B_Y+M_Y)$ is log canonical and $$A_{X,B+M}(E)\\le A_{Y,B_Y+M_Y}(E)$$ for every prime divisor $E$ over their common function field.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-integral-jump",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a positive dimension. Consider a sequence of smooth projective varieties $F$ over algebraically closed fields isomorphic to $\\mathbb C$, with projective birational morphisms $F\\to F_0$ to normal varieties. Let $\\eta_F$ be an lc rational volume form, and let $L$ be the pullback of a big semiample effective $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $F_0$. Suppose the supports of $\\operatorname{div}(\\eta_F)$ and $L$ on $F$ have simple normal crossings. Write $l_w=w(L)$ and assume the following conditions.*\n\n1.  *For every divisorial valuation $w$, the equality $A_{\\eta_F}(w)=0$ implies $l_w>0$.*\n\n2.  *For every prime divisor $P$ on $F_0$, $$A_{\\eta_F}(P)=1\\quad\\text{if }l_P=0,\n          \\qquad\n          A_{\\eta_F}(P)\\leq\\epsilon l_P\\quad\\text{if }l_P>0,$$ where $\\epsilon\\to0$ along the sequence.*\n\n3.  *For every rational $0<b\\leq1/10$, $h^0(F,K_F+\\lceil bL\\rceil)=1$.*\n\n4.  *There is a uniform constant $C$ such that every effective $\\mathbb Q$-divisor $J\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}L$ satisfies $w(J)\\leq C l_w$ at every divisorial valuation with $A_{\\eta_F}(w)=0$.*\n\n\n*Then, after passage to a subsequence, there is a uniform constant $C'$ such that every such $J$ and every divisorial valuation $w$ satisfy $$\\label{prod:integral-jump-bound}\n w(J)\\leq C'\\bigl(A_{\\eta_F}(w)+l_w\\bigr).$$ It suffices that the hypotheses hold sufficiently far along the sequence.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-integral-jump",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a positive dimension. Write $l_w=w(L)$ and assume the following conditions.*\n\n1.  *For every divisorial valuation $w$, the equality $A_{\\eta_F}(w)=0$ implies $l_w>0$.*\n\n2.  *For every prime divisor $P$ on $F_0$, $$A_{\\eta_F}(P)=1\\quad\\text{if }l_P=0,\n          \\qquad\n          A_{\\eta_F}(P)\\leq\\epsilon l_P\\quad\\text{if }l_P>0,$$ where $\\epsilon\\to0$ along the sequence.*\n\n3.  *For every rational $0<b\\leq1/10$, $h^0(F,K_F+\\lceil bL\\rceil)=1$.*\n\n4.  *There is a uniform constant $C$ such that every effective $\\mathbb Q$-divisor $J\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}L$ satisfies $w(J)\\leq C l_w$ at every divisorial valuation with $A_{\\eta_F}(w)=0$.*\n\n\n*Then, after passage to a subsequence, there is a uniform constant $C'$ such that every such $J$ and every divisorial valuation $w$ satisfy $$\\label{prod:integral-jump-bound}\n w(J)\\leq C'\\bigl(A_{\\eta_F}(w)+l_w\\bigr).$$ It suffices that the hypotheses hold sufficiently far along the sequence.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix a positive dimension. Let $\\eta_F$ be an lc rational volume form, and let $L$ be the pullback of a big semiample effective $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $F_0$. Write $l_w=w(L)$ and assume the following conditions.*\n\n1.  *For every divisorial valuation $w$, the equality $A_{\\eta_F}(w)=0$ implies $l_w>0$.*\n\n2.  *For every prime divisor $P$ on $F_0$, $$A_{\\eta_F}(P)=1\\quad\\text{if }l_P=0,\n          \\qquad\n          A_{\\eta_F}(P)\\leq\\epsilon l_P\\quad\\text{if }l_P>0,$$ where $\\epsilon\\to0$ along the sequence.*\n\n3.  *For every rational $0<b\\leq1/10$, $h^0(F,K_F+\\lceil bL\\rceil)=1$.*\n\n4.  *There is a uniform constant $C$ such that every effective $\\mathbb Q$-divisor $J\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}L$ satisfies $w(J)\\leq C l_w$ at every divisorial valuation with $A_{\\eta_F}(w)=0$.*\n\n\n*Then, after passage to a subsequence, there is a uniform constant $C'$ such that every such $J$ and every divisorial valuation $w$ satisfy $$\\label{prod:integral-jump-bound}\n w(J)\\leq C'\\bigl(A_{\\eta_F}(w)+l_w\\bigr).$$ It suffices that the hypotheses hold sufficiently far along the sequence.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prod-graph-bound",
        "exact_quote": "At every prime divisor $P$ of $F_0$, with its primitive valuation, put $c_P=P(D_F)$.       },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-val-gauss-tools",
        "exact_quote": "The following facts hold for an $n$-dimensional function field in characteristic zero.*\n\n\n1.  *If $w$ is Gauss on a full tuple $t_1,\\ldots,t_n$, then it is quasi-monomial, and $$\\label{val:log-frame}\n     A_\\eta(w)=w\\!\\left(\n     \\frac{\\eta}{d\\log t_1\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge d\\log t_n}\\right).$$ This identity is compatible with finite field extension and pullback of the form.*\n\n2.  *Suppose $w$ is quasi-monomial and Gauss on a partial tuple $f$. For nearby assignments of real weights to $f$, there are quasi-monomial Gauss extensions whose discrepancies and values on any prescribed finite subset of $L^*$ tend to those of $w$. If $w$ is centred at $o$, the extensions can be kept centred there.*\n\n3.  *On the finite cone complex of a smooth proper SNC model, the evaluation of a fixed rational function is continuous, homogeneous, and rational piecewise linear, including on faces.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-val-gauss-tools",
          "exact_quote": "The following facts hold for an $n$-dimensional function field in characteristic zero.*\n\n\n1.  *If $w$ is Gauss on a full tuple $t_1,\\ldots,t_n$, then it is quasi-monomial, and $$\\label{val:log-frame}\n     A_\\eta(w)=w\\!\\left(\n     \\frac{\\eta}{d\\log t_1\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge d\\log t_n}\\right).$$ This identity is compatible with finite field extension and pullback of the form.*\n\n2.  *Suppose $w$ is quasi-monomial and Gauss on a partial tuple $f$. For nearby assignments of real weights to $f$, there are quasi-monomial Gauss extensions whose discrepancies and values on any prescribed finite subset of $L^*$ tend to those of $w$. If $w$ is centred at $o$, the extensions can be kept centred there.*\n\n3.  *On the finite cone complex of a smooth proper SNC model, the evaluation of a fixed rational function is continuous, homogeneous, and rational piecewise linear, including on faces.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The following facts hold for an $n$-dimensional function field in characteristic zero.*\n\n\n1.  *If $w$ is Gauss on a full tuple $t_1,\\ldots,t_n$, then it is quasi-monomial, and $$\\label{val:log-frame}\n     A_\\eta(w)=w\\!\\left(\n     \\frac{\\eta}{d\\log t_1\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge d\\log t_n}\\right).$$ This identity is compatible with finite field extension and pullback of the form.*\n\n2.  *Suppose $w$ is quasi-monomial and Gauss on a partial tuple $f$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-val-optimization",
        "exact_quote": "The minimum is attained on a finite rational polyhedral subdivision of the SNC cone complex of a fixed smooth model. There are finitely many invariant regular functions, called detectors, such that maximizing the sum of their values among the minimizers permits the choice of a vertex. At this selected vertex, $$\\label{val:coefficient-vectors}\n w(h)=m_h\\cdot b\\quad(h\\in L^*),\\qquad\n A_\\eta(w)=e\\cdot b,\\qquad s(w)=g_s\\cdot b,$$ where all coefficient vectors are rational and have bounded denominators for this fixed vertex. Its rational rank is $r$. For an active generator, meaning $w(h_i)=s(w)v_i$, one has $m_{h_i}=v_i g_s$.*\n\n*For fixed germ and tuple, selection is described by finitely many piecewise semialgebraic branches as $b$ varies. On a segment starting at independent weights, these branches have a finite partition into intervals on which the selected ratio is rational-smooth. At dependent weights the polyhedral prescription is initially relaxed, without an extra Gauss condition. A compact limit of bounded-ratio selected vertices from independent positive weights remains a centred quasi-monomial Gauss extension at the limiting weights.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-val-optimization",

Its rational rank is $r$. For an active generator, meaning $w(h_i)=s(w)v_i$, one has $m_{h_i}=v_i g_s$.*\n\n*For fixed germ and tuple, selection is described by finitely many piecewise semialgebraic branches as $b$ varies.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-car-output",
        "exact_quote": "There is a spread of the algebraic data, retaining the given weights and cutoffs, such that, for all sufficiently large reductions of characteristic $p$, there is a single product $H$ of regular eigenfunctions with $$\\sigma=m_H=p\\phi g_s+O(1).$$ The error depends on the fixed characteristic-zero data but not on $p$. For this $H$ and every vector of nonnegative integers $(j_\\nu)$, there are a regular nonzero eigenfunction $R$ and integers $0\\le k_i<p$ with $$\\begin{aligned}\n p m_R&=\\sigma+\\sum_\\nu j_\\nu m_{t_\\nu}+k-(p-1)e,\n \\label{car:C2-value}\\\\\n p\\chi_R&=\\chi_H+\\sum_\\nu j_\\nu\\chi_{t_\\nu}\n                   +\\chi_{f^k}-(p-1)\\chi_\\eta.\n \\label{car:C2-character}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Characters are written additively. For every preassigned cutoff $T$, any collection of these reduced regular eigenfunctions with distinct pairs <span class=\"upright\">(value, character)</span> and weights at most $s(w_{\\rm orig})T$ has size at most the characteristic-zero number $\\mathcal H(T)$ of Lemma <a href=\"#val:window\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"val:window\">12</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-car-output",
          "exact_quote": "There is a spread of the algebraic data, retaining the given weights and cutoffs, such that, for all sufficiently large reductions of characteristic $p$, there is a single product $H$ of regular eigenfunctions with $$\\sigma=m_H=p\\phi g_s+O(1).$$ The error depends on the fixed characteristic-zero data but not on $p$. For this $H$ and every vector of nonnegative integers $(j_\\nu)$, there are a regular nonzero eigenfunction $R$ and integers $0\\le k_i<p$ with $$\\begin{aligned}\n p m_R&=\\sigma+\\sum_\\nu j_\\nu m_{t_\\nu}+k-(p-1)e,\n \\label{car:C2-value}\\\\\n p\\chi_R&=\\chi_H+\\sum_\\nu j_\\nu\\chi_{t_\\nu}\n                   +\\chi_{f^k}-(p-1)\\chi_\\eta.\n \\label{car:C2-character}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Characters are written additively. For every preassigned cutoff $T$, any collection of these reduced regular eigenfunctions with distinct pairs <span class=\"upright\">(value, character)</span> and weights at most $s(w_{\\rm orig})T$ has size at most the characteristic-zero number $\\mathcal H(T)$ of Lemma <a href=\"#val:window\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"val:window\">12</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.3"

      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a spread of the algebraic data, retaining the given weights and cutoffs, such that, for all sufficiently large reductions of characteristic $p$, there is a single product $H$ of regular eigenfunctions with $$\\sigma=m_H=p\\phi g_s+O(1).$$ The error depends on the fixed characteristic-zero data but not on $p$. For this $H$ and every vector of nonnegative integers $(j_\\nu)$, there are a regular nonzero eigenfunction $R$ and integers $0\\le k_i<p$ with $$\\begin{aligned}\n p m_R&=\\sigma+\\sum_\\nu j_\\nu m_{t_\\nu}+k-(p-1)e,\n \\label{car:C2-value}\\\\\n p\\chi_R&=\\chi_H+\\sum_\\nu j_\\nu\\chi_{t_\\nu}\n                   +\\chi_{f^k}-(p-1)\\chi_\\eta.\n \\label{car:C2-character}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Characters are written additively. For every preassigned cutoff $T$, any collection of these reduced regular eigenfunctions with distinct pairs <span class=\"upright\">(value, character)</span> and weights at most $s(w_{\\rm orig})T$ has size at most the characteristic-zero number $\\mathcal H(T)$ of Lemma <a href=\"#val:window\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"val:window\">12</a>.",
      "explanation": "After reduction to large positive characteristic, the logarithmic Cartier operator produces regular eigenfunctions whose values and characters satisfy exact affine relations with bounded counts.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "source_id": "PM-036.3"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ij-mixing-item",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:lifting-mixing\" label=\"thm:lifting-mixing\"></span> Fix positive integers $d,r$ and a positive real number $C$. The following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"ij:lifting-item\" label=\"ij:lifting-item\"></span> Let $P/Q$ be a regular extension with $\\operatorname{trdeg}_{\\mathbb C}P\\le d$, having a projective Fano type generic model. Let $\\sigma,\\psi$ be forms on $P$ whose boundaries on this model are effective, with $\\sigma$ of index at most $r$. Suppose, on every nonzero divisorial valuation of $P$, that $$A_\\psi>0,\\qquad 0\\le A_\\sigma\\le C A_\\psi.$$ For every $\\lambda>0$ there is $\\delta=\\delta(d,r,C,\\lambda)>0$ with the following property. If $u$ is a nonzero integral-valued divisorial valuation of $Q$ and $$\\inf_{w|_Q=u}A_\\psi(w)<\\delta,$$ then there is an integral-valued divisorial valuation $v$ of $P$ whose restriction is a positive multiple of $u$ and for which $A_\\psi(v)<\\lambda$. In relative dimension zero the regular extension is $P=Q$, and the same assertion holds.*\n\n2.  *Let $P$ have a projective Fano type model over $\\mathbb C$ of dimension at most $d$. Let $\\sigma,\\psi,\\chi$ be forms with effective boundaries on that model, with $\\sigma$ of index at most $r$, and suppose $$A_\\psi>0,\\qquad 0\\le A_\\sigma\\le C A_\\psi.$$ Suppose in addition that, for some $c>0$, $$A_\\chi(w)\\ge c A_\\psi(w)\n     \\quad\\text{whenever }w\\ne0\\text{ and }A_\\sigma(w)=0.$$ There is a rational number $a\\in(0,1)$ depending only on $d,r,C,c$ such that $$(1-a)A_\\psi(w)+aA_\\chi(w)>0\n     \\quad\\text{for every nonzero divisorial }w.$$*\n\n*All infima and homogeneous inequalities allow positive rational scalings of prime divisorial valuations. An integral-valued valuation is not required to be primitive.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ij-mixing-item",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:lifting-mixing\" label=\"thm:lifting-mixing\"></span> Fix positive integers $d,r$ and a positive real number $C$. If $u$ is a nonzero integral-valued divisorial valuation of $Q$ and $$\\inf_{w|_Q=u}A_\\psi(w)<\\delta,$$ then there is an integral-valued divisorial valuation $v$ of $P$ whose restriction is a positive multiple of $u$ and for which $A_\\psi(v)<\\lambda$. An integral-valued valuation is not required to be primitive.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tower-bounded-mixture",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:bounded-fibre\" label=\"thm:bounded-fibre\"></span> Let $L/K$ be a regular extension of finitely generated fields over $\\mathbb C$, with $\\operatorname{trdeg}_{\\mathbb C}L\\le d$. Suppose it has a positive-dimensional projective generic model $G/K$ that is geometrically $\\epsilon_0$-lc Fano, where $\\epsilon_0>0$ is fixed: $-K_G$ is ample and $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier, and the geometric underlying variety is $\\epsilon_0$-lc. Let $\\sigma$ be a form on $L$ of index at most $r$, with effective boundary on $G$ and with $A_\\sigma\\ge0$ on all absolute divisorial valuations of $L$. For every nonzero divisorial valuation $u$ of $K$, set $$\\bar A_\\sigma(u)=\\inf_{w|_K=u}A_\\sigma(w),$$ and use the same notation for other forms.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"tower:bounded-reference\" label=\"tower:bounded-reference\"></span> There is a form $\\sigma_K$ on $K$, with index bounded only in terms of $d,\\epsilon_0,r$, such that $$\\tfrac12\\bar A_\\sigma(u)\n     \\le A_{\\sigma_K}(u)\\le\\bar A_\\sigma(u)\n     \\quad\\text{for every nonzero divisorial }u.$$*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"tower:bounded-lift\" label=\"tower:bounded-lift\"></span> Let $\\psi$ be a form on $L$ with effective boundary on $G$, $A_\\psi>0$ on all nonzero absolute divisorial valuations, and $A_\\sigma\\le C A_\\psi$. For every $\\lambda>0$, there is $\\delta>0$, depending only on $d,\\epsilon_0,r,C,\\lambda$, such that an integral-valued nonzero divisorial valuation $u$ of $K$ with $\\bar A_\\psi(u)<\\delta$ has an integral-valued divisorial lift $v$ on $L$ satisfying $$v|_K\\in\\mathbb Q_{>0}u,\\qquad A_\\psi(v)<\\lambda.$$*\n\n3.  *Let $\\chi,\\psi$ be forms with effective boundaries on $G$; neither is required to be absolutely lc in this part. Suppose that, for a fixed $c>0$, $$A_\\chi(w)\\ge cA_\\psi(w)\n     \\quad\\text{on all nonzero divisorial }w\n     \\text{ with }A_\\sigma(w)=0.$$ There is $D\\ge0$, depending only on $d,\\epsilon_0,r,c$, such that $$\\label{tower:bounded-mixture-inequality}\n     A_\\chi(w)\\ge cA_\\psi(w)-D A_\\sigma(w)$$ whenever $w$ is trivial on $K$, or $u=w|_K$ is nonzero and $\\bar A_\\sigma(u)=0$.*\n\n*The boundaries on $G$ are interpreted by dividing out a base volume form as above. All comparisons allow positive rational scalings, and integral-valued valuations need not be primitive. If $K=\\mathbb C$, the first part has no nonzero valuations to test and a constant form of index one suffices; the second part is vacuous, while the third part includes every nonzero divisorial valuation of $L$.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-tower-bounded-mixture",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:bounded-fibre\" label=\"thm:bounded-fibre\"></span> Let $L/K$ be a regular extension of finitely generated fields over $\\mathbb C$, with $\\operatorname{trdeg}_{\\mathbb C}L\\le d$. Suppose it has a positive-dimensional projective generic model $G/K$ that is geometrically $\\epsilon_0$-lc Fano, where $\\epsilon_0>0$ is fixed: $-K_G$ is ample and $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier, and the geometric underlying variety is $\\epsilon_0$-lc. For every $\\lambda>0$, there is $\\delta>0$, depending only on $d,\\epsilon_0,r,C,\\lambda$, such that an integral-valued nonzero divisorial valuation $u$ of $K$ with $\\bar A_\\psi(u)<\\delta$ has an integral-valued divisorial lift $v$ on $L$ satisfying $$v|_K\\in\\mathbb Q_{>0}u,\\qquad A_\\psi(v)<\\lambda.$$*\n\n3.  *Let $\\chi,\\psi$ be forms with effective boundaries on $G$; neither is required to be absolutely lc in this part. Suppose that, for a fixed $c>0$, $$A_\\chi(w)\\ge cA_\\psi(w)\n     \\quad\\text{on all nonzero divisorial }w\n     \\text{ with }A_\\sigma(w)=0.$$ There is $D\\ge0$, depending only on $d,\\epsilon_0,r,c$, such that $$\\label{tower:bounded-mixture-inequality}\n     A_\\chi(w)\\ge cA_\\psi(w)-D A_\\sigma(w)$$ whenever $w$ is trivial on $K$, or $u=w|_K$ is nonzero and $\\bar A_\\sigma(u)=0$.*\n\n*The boundaries on $G$ are interpreted by dividing out a base volume form as above. All comparisons allow positive rational scalings, and integral-valued valuations need not be primitive. If $K=\\mathbb C$, the first part has no nonzero valuations to test and a constant form of index one suffices; the second part is vacuous, while the third part includes every nonzero divisorial valuation of $L$.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"thm:bounded-fibre\" label=\"thm:bounded-fibre\"></span> Let $L/K$ be a regular extension of finitely generated fields over $\\mathbb C$, with $\\operatorname{trdeg}_{\\mathbb C}L\\le d$. Suppose it has a positive-dimensional projective generic model $G/K$ that is geometrically $\\epsilon_0$-lc Fano, where $\\epsilon_0>0$ is fixed: $-K_G$ is ample and $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier, and the geometric underlying variety is $\\epsilon_0$-lc. Let $\\sigma$ be a form on $L$ of index at most $r$, with effective boundary on $G$ and with $A_\\sigma\\ge0$ on all absolute divisorial valuations of $L$. For every nonzero divisorial valuation $u$ of $K$, set $$\\bar A_\\sigma(u)=\\inf_{w|_K=u}A_\\sigma(w),$$ and use the same notation for other forms.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"tower:bounded-reference\" label=\"tower:bounded-reference\"></span> There is a form $\\sigma_K$ on $K$, with index bounded only in terms of $d,\\epsilon_0,r$, such that $$\\tfrac12\\bar A_\\sigma(u)\n     \\le A_{\\sigma_K}(u)\\le\\bar A_\\sigma(u)\n     \\quad\\text{for every nonzero divisorial }u.$$*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"tower:bounded-lift\" label=\"tower:bounded-lift\"></span> Let $\\psi$ be a form on $L$ with effective boundary on $G$, $A_\\psi>0$ on all nonzero absolute divisorial valuations, and $A_\\sigma\\le C A_\\psi$. For every $\\lambda>0$, there is $\\delta>0$, depending only on $d,\\epsilon_0,r,C,\\lambda$, such that an integral-valued nonzero divisorial valuation $u$ of $K$ with $\\bar A_\\psi(u)<\\delta$ has an integral-valued divisorial lift $v$ on $L$ satisfying $$v|_K\\in\\mathbb Q_{>0}u,\\qquad A_\\psi(v)<\\lambda.$$*\n\n3.  *Let $\\chi,\\psi$ be forms with effective boundaries on $G$; neither is required to be absolutely lc in this part. Suppose that, for a fixed $c>0$, $$A_\\chi(w)\\ge cA_\\psi(w)\n     \\quad\\text{on all nonzero divisorial }w\n     \\text{ with }A_\\sigma(w)=0.$$ There is $D\\ge0$, depending only on $d,\\epsilon_0,r,c$, such that $$\\label{tower:bounded-mixture-inequality}\n     A_\\chi(w)\\ge cA_\\psi(w)-D A_\\sigma(w)$$ whenever $w$ is trivial on $K$, or $u=w|_K$ is nonzero and $\\bar A_\\sigma(u)=0$.*\n\n*The boundaries on $G$ are interpreted by dividing out a base volume form as above. All comparisons allow positive rational scalings, and integral-valued valuations need not be primitive. If $K=\\mathbb C$, the first part has no nonzero valuations to test and a constant form of index one suffices; the second part is vacuous, while the third part includes every nonzero divisorial valuation of $L$.",
      "explanation": "For bounded Fano generic fibres, a bounded-index base form approximates the barred discrepancy, integral valuations lift, and a mixture inequality holds with a uniform constant.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

          "source_id": "PM-036.3"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-tower-base-substitution",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L/K$ be a regular extension as above, and let $G/K$ be a normal geometrically integral projective generic model. Let $\\omega$ be a form whose boundary on $G$ is effective and sub-lc. Then the threshold canonical bundle formula gives a discriminant b-divisor and a b-Cartier b-semiample moduli b-divisor with generalized discrepancy $\\bar A_\\omega$. Each defining infimum for a nonzero divisorial base valuation is finite and attained.*\n\n\n*Suppose another such form $\\psi$ has $\\bar A_\\psi(u)>0$ for every nonzero divisorial $u$ of $K$. For every rational $\\gamma>0$, there is a form $\\omega_K$ on $K$ such that $$\\label{tower:substitution-bounds}\n \\bar A_\\omega(u)-\\gamma\\bar A_\\psi(u)\n \\le A_{\\omega_K}(u)\\le\\bar A_\\omega(u).$$ No index bound is asserted. The boundary of $\\omega$ need not be sub-lc at vertical valuations.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tower-geometric-step",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P/Q$ be a regular extension of positive transcendence degree having a projective Fano type model over $Q$. Suppose finitely many forms on $P$ have effective boundaries on this model. There is a projective Mori contraction $H\\to U$ over $Q$, with $Q(H)=P$ and $R=Q(U)$, such that:*\n\n1.  *$P/R$ and $R/Q$ are regular, and $\\operatorname{trdeg}_Q R<\\operatorname{trdeg}_Q P$;*\n\n2.  *the generic fibre of $H\\to U$ is positive-dimensional and geometrically $1/10$-lc Fano;*\n\n3.  *$H$ and $U$ are of Fano type over $Q$, and all the carried form boundaries remain effective on $H$.*\n\n*In addition, any base substitutions with discrepancy at most the appropriate barred discrepancy have effective traces on $U$.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-tower-geometric-step",

          "source_id": "PM-036.3"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bounded-preparation",
        "exact_quote": "The finitely many packages can be chosen with projective morphisms $f:Y\\to B$ having the following properties. The base is smooth and strict toroidal; the source is strict simplicial toroidal; and every source cone maps onto a base cone. The good parameter open is disjoint from the base boundary. For each field-valued point used in a package, $Y_K$ maps projectively and birationally to the prescribed $G$, and the differential and form identities specialize. The divisor of $\\eta$, regarded as a relative logarithmic pluri-top form, is toroidal. Arbitrary prescribed proper closed subsets can be included in the boundary during this construction.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-auto-remark-1",
        "exact_quote": "The preliminary saturation and the subsequent choice of labels cannot be omitted. On the open set $y(y^2+x)\\ne0$, the map $$t=x^2(y^2+x)$$ has boundary lattice map multiplication by two at the ordinary component $x=0$. Its generic fibre is geometrically integral: with $z=xy$, its equation is $z^2=t-x^3$. After $t=\\pi^2$, normalization introduces $q=\\pi/x$, with $$q^2=y^2+x,\\qquad \\pi=xq.$$ The special fibre over that old component has two primes, $q=y$ and $q=-y$. Both have boundary weights $w(x)=w(\\pi)=1$, $w(y)=0$, but their orders differ on $q-y$. The bounded Kummer normalization separates them into different new strata, each with primitive boundary map. Taking the labels on that new model is exactly what makes the uniqueness proved above applicable.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-auto-remark-1",

Taking the labels on that new model is exactly what makes the uniqueness proved above applicable.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.3"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-trait-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "For every parameter map allowed above and every nonzero divisorial base valuation $u$, the barred reference discrepancy is $$\\bar A_\\sigma(u)=A_\\zeta(u)+\\mu(b).\n \\tag{T}\\label{trait:T}$$ For a divisorial $w$ over $u$, with labelled monomial retraction $w_x$, there is an excess satisfying $$A_\\sigma(w)=A_\\zeta(u)+\\phi(x)+E(w),\\qquad\n E(w)\\ge0,\\qquad E(w_x)=0.\n \\tag{TE}\\label{trait:TE}$$ Let $\\omega$ be any other form with effective boundary on $G$, and compare it to $\\sigma$ at a common tensor index $p$, writing $$A_\\omega(v)=A_\\sigma(v)+\\frac{v(H)}p.$$ There is a uniform constant $C_2$ such that $$\\begin{split}\n |w(H)-w_x(H)|&\\le C_2pE(w),\\\\\n |w_x(H)-w_{x'}(H)|&\\le C_2p\\|x-x'\\|.\n \\end{split}\n \\tag{TV}\\label{trait:TV}$$ The second inequality holds along every segment in one labelled slice cell, including incident endpoints, in fixed cone coordinates. The rational monomial valuations in these assertions are the compatible algebraic labelled ray orders constructed above. Formulae <a href=\"#trait:T\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"trait:T\">[trait:T]</a> and <a href=\"#trait:TE\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"trait:TE\">[trait:TE]</a> do not require absolute log canonicity of $\\sigma$.",
        "source_id": "PM-036.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-trait-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "For every parameter map allowed above and every nonzero divisorial base valuation $u$, the barred reference discrepancy is $$\\bar A_\\sigma(u)=A_\\zeta(u)+\\mu(b).\n \\tag{T}\\label{trait:T}$$ For a divisorial $w$ over $u$, with labelled monomial retraction $w_x$, there is an excess satisfying $$A_\\sigma(w)=A_\\zeta(u)+\\phi(x)+E(w),\\qquad\n E(w)\\ge0,\\qquad E(w_x)=0.\n \\tag{TE}\\label{trait:TE}$$ Let $\\omega$ be any other form with effective boundary on $G$, and compare it to $\\sigma$ at a common tensor index $p$, writing $$A_\\omega(v)=A_\\sigma(v)+\\frac{v(H)}p.$$ There is a uniform constant $C_2$ such that $$\\begin{split}\n |w(H)-w_x(H)|&\\le C_2pE(w),\\\\\n |w_x(H)-w_{x'}(H)|&\\le C_2p\\|x-x'\\|.\n \\end{split}\n \\tag{TV}\\label{trait:TV}$$ The second inequality holds along every segment in one labelled slice cell, including incident endpoints, in fixed cone coordinates. The rational monomial valuations in these assertions are the compatible algebraic labelled ray orders constructed above. Formulae <a href=\"#trait:T\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"trait:T\">[trait:T]</a> and <a href=\"#trait:TE\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"trait:TE\">[trait:TE]</a> do not require absolute log canonicity of $\\sigma$.",
          "source_id": "PM-036.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every parameter map allowed above and every nonzero divisorial base valuation $u$, the barred reference discrepancy is $$\\bar A_\\sigma(u)=A_\\zeta(u)+\\mu(b).\n \\tag{T}\\label{trait:T}$$ For a divisorial $w$ over $u$, with labelled monomial retraction $w_x$, there is an excess satisfying $$A_\\sigma(w)=A_\\zeta(u)+\\phi(x)+E(w),\\qquad\n E(w)\\ge0,\\qquad E(w_x)=0.\n \\tag{TE}\\label{trait:TE}$$ Let $\\omega$ be any other form with effective boundary on $G$, and compare it to $\\sigma$ at a common tensor index $p$, writing $$A_\\omega(v)=A_\\sigma(v)+\\frac{v(H)}p.$$ There is a uniform constant $C_2$ such that $$\\begin{split}\n |w(H)-w_x(H)|&\\le C_2pE(w),\\\\\n |w_x(H)-w_{x'}(H)|&\\le C_2p\\|x-x'\\|.\n \\end{split}\n \\tag{TV}\\label{trait:TV}$$ The second inequality holds along every segment in one labelled slice cell, including incident endpoints, in fixed cone coordinates. The rational monomial valuations in these assertions are the compatible algebraic labelled ray orders constructed above. Formulae <a href=\"#trait:T\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"trait:T\">[trait:T]</a> and <a href=\"#trait:TE\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"trait:TE\">[trait:TE]</a> do not require absolute log canonicity of $\\sigma$.",
      "explanation": "The barred reference discrepancy equals the base discrepancy plus the toroidal minimum, and any prolongation differs from its monomial retraction by a nonnegative excess with Lipschitz control.",

        "exact_quote": "There is a smooth projective toroidal resolution $P\\to Y$ and a finite sequence of ordinary $\\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt MMP steps over $B$ from $P$ to a model $P_j$ with an effective dlt boundary $D_j$ such that $$K_{P_j}+D_j\\sim_{\\mathbb Q,B}0,$$ and $(P_j,D_j)$ is crepant to $(Y,C^\\natural)$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-lc-incidence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $s$ have nonzero base cone $\\tau_s$. Let $\\delta_0$ be a source face on which $g$ vanishes, let $V=\\overline{O_{\\delta_0}}$ be its closed stratum, and let $C_s$ be any connected component of $V_s$. The zero face is allowed, with $V=Y$. Then there is a source cone $\\beta$ such that $$g|_\\beta=0,\\qquad p_*(\\beta)=\\tau_s,\\qquad\n \\overline{O_\\beta}\\subset V,\\qquad O_\\beta\\cap C_s\\ne\\varnothing.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-036.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-lc-incidence",
          "exact_quote": "Let $s$ have nonzero base cone $\\tau_s$. Let $\\delta_0$ be a source face on which $g$ vanishes, let $V=\\overline{O_{\\delta_0}}$ be its closed stratum, and let $C_s$ be any connected component of $V_s$. The zero face is allowed, with $V=Y$. Then there is a source cone $\\beta$ such that $$g|_\\beta=0,\\qquad p_*(\\beta)=\\tau_s,\\qquad\n \\overline{O_\\beta}\\subset V,\\qquad O_\\beta\\cap C_s\\ne\\varnothing.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-036.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $s$ have nonzero base cone $\\tau_s$. Let $\\delta_0$ be a source face on which $g$ vanishes, let $V=\\overline{O_{\\delta_0}}$ be its closed stratum, and let $C_s$ be any connected component of $V_s$. The zero face is allowed, with $V=Y$. Then there is a source cone $\\beta$ such that $$g|_\\beta=0,\\qquad p_*(\\beta)=\\tau_s,\\qquad\n \\overline{O_\\beta}\\subset V,\\qquad O_\\beta\\cap C_s\\ne\\varnothing.$$",
      "explanation": "Every connected component of a zero-gap closed stratum fibre meets the stratum of a zero-gap cone mapping onto the base cone.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **NQC nef part** ([source](#pm-h-9)): An NQC nef part is a nonnegative real combination of nef rational Cartier b-divisors.
- **contraction** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A contraction is a projective surjective morphism $f$ to a normal variety with $f_*\mathcal O=\mathcal O$.
- **generalized discrepancy** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a prime divisor $E$ on $W$, set $$A_{X,B+M}(E)=1-\operatorname{coeff}_E(B_W).$$
- **divisorial valuation** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A divisorial valuation means a positive real multiple $v=c\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E$ of a prime-divisor order, unless an integral or rational normalization is specified.
- **Fano type** ([source](#sec:integral-jump)): A projective variety is of *Fano type* if it admits an effective klt boundary whose negative adjoint is ample.
- **Gauss on a tuple** ([source](#pm-h-31)): We say that a valuation is *Gauss on a tuple* when the tuple has algebraically independent homogeneous initials; equivalently, every nonzero polynomial in the tuple has the minimum of its monomial weights.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-cone-estimates** [claim](#stmt-prop-cone-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cone-estimates): Twisted valuation with volume comparison and inversion of adjunction -> threshold and comparison bounds on the cone
- **s-graph-bound** [claim](#stmt-prod-graph-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prod-graph-bound): Gauss extension and logarithmic differential wedge on the cone -> discrepancy bound on the normal graph
- **s-one-section** [claim](#stmt-prod-one-section) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prod-one-section): Adjunction lifting and Kawamata–Viehweg vanishing -> uniqueness of the adjoint section
- **s-window** [claim](#stmt-val-window) / [proof](#proof-stmt-val-window): Composite valuation and lattice index counting -> window bound on colength
- **s-gauss-tools** [claim](#stmt-val-gauss-tools) / [proof](#proof-stmt-val-gauss-tools): Newton polygon and log-frame calculation -> Gauss valuation tools
- **s-strict-retraction** [claim](#stmt-val-strict-retraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-val-strict-retraction): Toroidal subdivision and transverse divisor -> strict decrease under retraction
- **s-optimization** [claim](#stmt-val-optimization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-val-optimization): Compactness on polyhedral cone complex with detector functions -> selected rational vertex
- **s-strict-inequality** [claim](#stmt-car-strict-inequality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-car-strict-inequality): One-parameter variation of the optimal valuation -> strict positivity of the residual combination
- **s-cartier-output** [claim](#stmt-car-output) / [proof](#proof-stmt-car-strict-inequality-2): Logarithmic Cartier operator after reduction to large characteristic -> eigenfunctions with exact value relations
- **s-algebraic-counts** [claim](#stmt-round-algebraic-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-round-algebraic-counts): Disjoint boxes against the window bound -> uniform class counts
- **s-enlarge** [claim](#stmt-round-enlarge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-round-enlarge): Dimension count of monomial combinations -> tuple enlargement
- **s-derivative** [claim](#stmt-round-derivative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-round-derivative): Box counting for powers of the Cartier output -> ratio derivative bound
- **s-segment** [claim](#stmt-round-segment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-round-segment): Integration of the derivative bound along the segment with compact limits -> rounded Gauss extension
- **s-preparation** [claim](#stmt-bounded-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bounded-preparation): Weak toroidalization with cone refinement -> toroidal packages
- **s-trait-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-trait-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-trait-comparison): Trait chart and excess computation -> discrepancy identity and Lipschitz bounds
- **s-effective-model** [claim](#stmt-inc-effective-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inc-effective-model): MMP with scaling and very exceptional negativity -> effective dlt model
- **s-lc-incidence** [claim](#stmt-prop-lc-incidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lc-incidence): Linking of lc centres on the dlt model -> incident zero-gap cone
- **s-base-substitution** [claim](#stmt-tower-base-substitution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tower-base-substitution): Canonical bundle formula and general moduli member -> ordinary base forms
- **s-geometric-step** [claim](#stmt-tower-geometric-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tower-geometric-step): Extraction of low discrepancy places and Mori fibre space -> Fano type tower step
- **s-lifting-mixing** [claim](#stmt-ij-mixing-item) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tower-geometric-step-2): Induction on transcendence degree using the bounded-fibre estimate -> lifting and mixing
- **s-integral-jump** [claim](#stmt-prop-integral-jump) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ij-mixing-item): Integral barrier and mixture of forms -> integral jump bound
- **s-phase-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-phase) / [proof](#proof-stmt-round-segment-2): Linear dependence modulo high valuation ideals and torsion clearing -> phase congruence
- **s-smooth-termination** [claim](#stmt-prop-smooth-termination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-smooth-termination): Contradiction via countable limits, common index and late vanishing -> termination with nef canonical divisor
- **s-relative-wzd** [claim](#stmt-prop-relative-wzd) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-relative-wzd): Double cover and equivariant averaging -> relative weak Zariski decomposition
- **s-field-descent** [claim](#stmt-prop-field-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-field-descent): Spreading out and specialization -> descent to any characteristic-zero field
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-17): Relative weak decomposition upgraded by terminating generalized program -> existence form of the main theorem

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-target-congruence**: For the finite rounding step of the construction, each output target b*_j is a rational number such that S times b*_j is congruent to the phase of the j-th character angle modulo the integers.
- **inv-nearest-target-distance**: The chosen target for each new coordinate is the nearest admissible one, so its distance from the unrounded weight is at most one over twice S.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-rounding-is-not-lemma-21**: Treat the executed nearest-target rounding as a verification of the paper's rounding lemma or of the phase theorem. -> The code only checks the one-dimensional arithmetic of choosing a nearest admissible rational; it says nothing about Gauss extensions, the ratio budget along the segment, or the congruence for all semi-invariants.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The main theorems are existence results with no finite procedure. The one explicit finite step in the paper is the choice, for each new coordinate, of the nearest target satisfying the stated congruence; this exact rational computation is what the harness executes.

```python
# INPUTS: S positive integer, m count, then m pairs (b_j, theta_j) of exact fractions, all whitespace separated on stdin
# PRECONDITIONS: S>=1, m>=1, every token parses as an exact fraction
# OUTPUTS: m space-separated exact fractions, the nearest admissible targets (ties go to the larger target)
# TIME: O(m) fraction operations
# SPACE: O(m)
# ARITHMETIC: exact rationals via fractions, no floating point
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and the process exits nonzero
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def main():
    toks = sys.stdin.read().split()
    S = int(toks[0])
    m = int(toks[1])
    out = []
    for j in range(m):
        b = Fraction(toks[2 + 2 * j])
        th = Fraction(toks[3 + 2 * j])
        x = S * b - th
        k = (2 * x.numerator + x.denominator) // (2 * x.denominator)
        out.append((th + k) / S)
    print(" ".join(str(v) for v in out))

main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorem is an existence statement for a birational model of a projective generalized log canonical pair. It supplies no finite procedure, data format or interface a program could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper works with abstract varieties and birational maps over algebraically closed fields. It has no state space, dynamics or stochastic model that a simulation could use. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The conclusion is a contraction or a nef model whose existence is proved, not a computable output. No checker, converter or developer tool follows from it. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The termination argument is a proof by contradiction that uses countability of limiting discrepancy changes. It gives no explicit steps and no complexity bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite cone complexes occur only as auxiliary objects inside valuation arguments over models of function fields. They are not offered as a representation with operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The local phase theorem concerns cyclic group actions on algebraic germs. It has no connection to circuits, memory or parallel dataflow. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Reduction modulo large primes appears only as a proof device for logarithmic Cartier selection. It is not used to build a hard problem or a protocol. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only uniformity statements are sequential, along a subsequence of germs. There is no data, loss function or learning guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Valuation optimization minimizes a ratio over a polyhedral subdivision of a cone complex. It is an existence argument for one vertex, not a solver or a modelled decision problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The scaled canonical program is a sequence of birational steps on one variety. It has no processes, messages or fault model. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No standalone applied domain is justified. The value of the result is internal to birational geometry, as a pseudo-effective alternative and a Mori fibre space alternative. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-field-and-pair-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The existence theorem is stated only for projective generalized log canonical Q-pairs over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, with the standing description of the pair given in the theorem. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to pairs over fields of positive or mixed characteristic', 'Applying the theorem to non-projective varieties or to pairs that are not generalized log canonical']
- **limit-fixed-nef-data** (hypothesis): The nef data are kept fixed throughout every birational step, so the result gives no freedom to vary or deform the nef b-divisor along the program. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem for programs that change or perturb the nef part between steps', 'Treating the nef part as a free parameter in the existence statement']
- **limit-nqc-transfer** (formal_scope): The terminating transfer to the final pair is carried out in the NQC setting, which is available because the fixed nef data are rational. Blocked use: ['Applying the transfer step to generalized pairs whose nef data are not rational or not NQC']
- **limit-existence-not-arbitrary-mmp** (nonconstructive): The theorem produces a terminating choice of birational program. It does not say that an arbitrary sequence of contractions and flips terminates, and the termination proof is by contradiction, so it yields no explicit procedure. Blocked use: ['Claiming that any run of the program terminates', 'Extracting an algorithm or a bound on the number of steps from the proof']
- **limit-no-semiampleness-generalized** (formal_scope): The endpoint is shown to be nef only. For nonzero generalized nef data the paper claims no semiampleness of the adjoint divisor. Blocked use: ['Deducing semiampleness or finite generation of section rings for nonzero generalized nef data', 'Using the nef endpoint as if it carried a nonzero pluricanonical section']
- **limit-phase-germ-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The local phase assertion concerns a sequence of pointed Gorenstein klt germs of fixed dimension at least two with closed points, with cyclic group actions and a semi-invariant canonical generator; the conclusion is only along a subsequence. Blocked use: ['Applying the phase congruence to non-Gorenstein germs or non-klt singularities', 'Applying it in dimension below two or to non-cyclic group actions', 'Reading the conclusion as holding for the full sequence rather than a subsequence']
- **limit-subsequence-uniformity** (approximation): Uniform constants in the sequential estimates are obtained only after passing to a subsequence, and each assertion specifies which earlier choices may enter the bound. Blocked use: ['Reading the uniform constants as explicit or effective bounds valid for every germ in the original sequence', 'Using the constants without tracking which earlier choices they depend on']
- **limit-vertex-denominator-and-characteristic** (edge_case): The denominator of the selected vertex and the required reduction characteristic depend on the fixed germ, the finite tests and the cutoffs, and no uniform bound on their complexity is asserted. Blocked use: ['Treating the reduction characteristic as a universal constant independent of the germ', 'Bounding the combinatorial complexity of the selected vertex uniformly along the sequence']
- **limit-cartier-characteristic-size** (approximation): The logarithmic Cartier selection is made for fixed characteristic-zero data and arbitrarily large characteristic, but the required size of the characteristic is not uniform among germs or choices of tuples. Blocked use: ['Fixing a single prime to run the Cartier selection for all germs', 'Presenting the positive-characteristic step as a uniform reduction']
- **limit-saturation-and-labels** (counterexample_scope): Without the preliminary saturation and the choice of labels on the normalized model, distinct primes in the special fibre can share boundary weights yet have different orders, so uniqueness of the ray order fails. Blocked use: ['Identifying a valuation by its boundary weights alone on a non-saturated model', 'Skipping the Kummer normalization before comparing ray orders']
- **limit-complex-base-field-local** (formal_scope): Local and field-theoretic arguments take place over the complex numbers unless another field is specified; passage to other algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero is a separate descent step. Blocked use: ['Running the local valuative arguments directly over an arbitrary field without the descent step']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We resolve the existence form of the minimal-model conjecture for projective generalized log canonical ℚ-pairs over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero. Such a pair admits a minimal model when its adjoint divisor is pseudo-effective, and a Mori fibre space otherwise, while keeping the nef ℚ-Cartier b-divisor fixed. Taking the nef part to be zero gives the corresponding result for ordinary log canonical ℚ-pairs.
